- [ ] **Step 3: Give the Azure Monitor endpoint an optional name**

  Replace `modules/monitorPrivateEndpoint.bicep` (the default keeps `ampls-defenstack-<env>-pe`; no length decorator, because `@maxLength` on an interpolated default raises BCP335):

  ```bicep
  // The platform's single Azure Monitor private endpoint, in the primary spoke's private-endpoint subnet. Its NSG
  // already admits HTTPS from the approved sources, and the shared zones make it resolvable from every linked VNet.
  // The endpoint joins the spoke's AMPLS application security group, the only destination the NSG opens to the
  // peer region's firewall-SNATed traffic (ADR-022). During a declared outage, failover.bicep adds a second endpoint
  // in the East US spoke under its own name; its zone group re-points the shared records there (ADR-032).
  import { privateDnsZoneSet } from 'types.bicep'

  @description('Azure region for the private endpoint (the primary region).')
  param location string

  @description('Azure Monitor Private Link Scope resource ID.')
  param privateLinkScopeId string

  @description('Azure Monitor Private Link Scope name, used for deterministic private endpoint naming.')
  @minLength(1)
  @maxLength(255)
  param privateLinkScopeName string

  @description('Private endpoint name. The default keeps the Phase 6 primary endpoint name; failover.bicep names its East US endpoint.')
  param privateEndpointName string = '${privateLinkScopeName}-pe'

  @description('Private-endpoint subnet resource ID in the primary spoke.')
  param privateEndpointSubnetId string

  @description('Resource IDs of the shared private DNS zones created by the global layer.')
  param privateDnsZoneIds privateDnsZoneSet

  @description('Resource ID of the primary spoke\'s AMPLS application security group, which the endpoint joins.')
  param applicationSecurityGroupId string

  resource privateEndpoint 'Microsoft.Network/privateEndpoints@2024-07-01' = {
    name: privateEndpointName
    location: location
    properties: {
      privateLinkServiceConnections: [
        {
          name: 'azuremonitor'
          properties: {
            privateLinkServiceId: privateLinkScopeId
            groupIds: [
              'azuremonitor'
            ]
          }
        }
      ]
      subnet: {
        id: privateEndpointSubnetId
      }
      applicationSecurityGroups: [
        {
          id: applicationSecurityGroupId
        }
      ]
    }
  }

  // Azure Monitor needs its four zones plus the shared blob zone (agent solution packs); the blob records are
  // per storage account, so they sit beside the application's own blob records without conflict.
  resource privateDnsZoneGroup 'Microsoft.Network/privateEndpoints/privateDnsZoneGroups@2024-07-01' = {
    parent: privateEndpoint
    name: 'default'
    properties: {
      privateDnsZoneConfigs: [
        {
          name: 'monitor'
          properties: {
            privateDnsZoneId: privateDnsZoneIds.monitor
          }
        }
        {
          name: 'oms'
          properties: {
            privateDnsZoneId: privateDnsZoneIds.oms
          }
        }
        {
          name: 'ods'
          properties: {
            privateDnsZoneId: privateDnsZoneIds.ods
          }
        }
        {
          name: 'agentsvc'
          properties: {
            privateDnsZoneId: privateDnsZoneIds.agentsvc
          }
        }
        {
          name: 'blob'
          properties: {
            privateDnsZoneId: privateDnsZoneIds.blob
          }
        }
      ]
    }
  }

  output id string = privateEndpoint.id
  ```

- [ ] **Step 4: Create the failover modules**

  Create `modules/failover/appServicePlanScale.bicep` (only the plan; reusing `appService.bicep` would redeploy the site):

  ```bicep
  // Scales the warm-standby App Service plan to prod capacity during a declared outage (Phase 8, runbook 08). It
  // re-states the plan exactly as appService.bicep created it, changing only the instance count, so the application
  // is not redeployed. The East US plan was created without zone redundancy, which cannot change after creation.

  @description('Azure region of the existing plan.')
  param location string

  @description('Name of the existing App Service plan.')
  @minLength(1)
  @maxLength(60)
  param appServicePlanName string

  @description('Instance count during the failover.')
  @minValue(1)
  @maxValue(30)
  param capacity int

  resource appServicePlan 'Microsoft.Web/serverfarms@2025-03-01' = {
    name: appServicePlanName
    location: location
    // The prod SKU from appService.bicep; a test pins the parity.
    sku: {
      name: 'P2V3'
      tier: 'PremiumV3'
      capacity: capacity
    }
    properties: {
      zoneRedundant: false
    }
  }

  output name string = appServicePlan.name
  ```

  Create `modules/failover/storagePrivateEndpoint.bicep`:

  ```bicep
  // Write access to the prod primary app storage account from the East US warm standby during a declared outage
  // (Phase 8, runbook 08). The account's own <account>-pe endpoint sits in the West US 3 spoke, which is unreachable,
  // so this endpoint gives the East US spoke a blob path to the account after its failover. It mirrors the storage
  // endpoint of privateConnectivity.bicep; a test pins the parity. Its zone group overwrites the account's A record in
  // the shared blob zone; failback deletes this endpoint and the next pipeline deploy restores the West US 3 record.

  @description('Azure region for the private endpoint (the warm-standby region).')
  param location string

  @description('Resource ID of the prod primary app storage account.')
  param storageAccountId string

  @description('Private endpoint name. It must differ from <account>-pe and <account>-secondary-pe.')
  @minLength(2)
  @maxLength(80)
  param privateEndpointName string

  @description('Private endpoint subnet in the warm-standby spoke.')
  param privateEndpointSubnetId string

  @description('Shared privatelink.blob zone resource ID.')
  param blobPrivateDnsZoneId string

  resource storagePrivateEndpoint 'Microsoft.Network/privateEndpoints@2024-07-01' = {
    name: privateEndpointName
    location: location
    properties: {
      privateLinkServiceConnections: [
        {
          name: 'blob'
          properties: {
            privateLinkServiceId: storageAccountId
            groupIds: [
              'blob'
            ]
          }
        }
      ]
      subnet: {
        id: privateEndpointSubnetId
      }
    }
  }

  // Registers the endpoint in the shared blob zone, re-pointing <account>.privatelink.blob to East US.
  resource storagePrivateDnsZoneGroup 'Microsoft.Network/privateEndpoints/privateDnsZoneGroups@2024-07-01' = {
    parent: storagePrivateEndpoint
    name: 'default'
    properties: {
      privateDnsZoneConfigs: [
        {
          name: 'blob'
          properties: {
            privateDnsZoneId: blobPrivateDnsZoneId
          }
        }
      ]
    }
  }

  output id string = storagePrivateEndpoint.id
  output name string = storagePrivateEndpoint.name
  ```

- [ ] **Step 5: Create the entry point and its parameter file**

  Create `failover.bicep`:

  ```bicep
  // Regional failover (Phase 8): brings the prod East US warm standby up to primary strength during a declared West US 3
  // outage. An operator deploys it with az deployment sub create (runbook 08); main.bicep and the pipeline never do, and
  // the pipeline stays frozen until failback, since a pipeline run would undo these changes (ADR-032).
  //
  // It deploys into the East US resource group only. It reads the shared private DNS zones, the workspace and the AMPLS
  // from the global resource group, and the endpoints' zone groups write records into those zones. Nothing here targets
  // the West US 3 resource group: West US 3 appears only in the resource ID of the primary storage account, which the
  // East US blob endpoint connects to. Tests pin both.
  targetScope = 'subscription'

  @description('Environment that fails over. Only prod has a warm standby.')
  @allowed([
    'prod'
  ])
  param environmentName string = 'prod'

  @description('Region that takes over: the warm-standby region.')
  @allowed([
    'eastus'
  ])
  param location string = 'eastus'

  @description('Instance count for the East US App Service plan during the failover. 3 matches the prod primary plan.')
  @minValue(1)
  @maxValue(30)
  param appServicePlanCapacity int = 3

  @description('Resource ID of the prod primary app storage account, which the East US blob endpoint connects to. The default names it as main.bicep and regionStamp.bicep do (rg-defenstack-<env>-wus3, st<code><nameSuffix>); a test pins the parity.')
  param primaryStorageAccountId string = resourceId(
    subscription().subscriptionId,
    'rg-defenstack-${environmentName}-wus3',
    'Microsoft.Storage/storageAccounts',
    'stwus3${uniqueString(subscription().id, environmentName, 'westus3')}'
  )

  @description('Confirms a declared outage. False deploys nothing, so a stray run outside DR changes nothing.')
  param failoverActive bool = true

  // The same region code and shared zone names as main.bicep; tests pin the parity.
  var regionCode = 'eus'
  var resourceGroupName = 'rg-defenstack-${environmentName}-${regionCode}'
  var globalResourceGroupName = 'rg-defenstack-${environmentName}-global'
  var privateDnsZoneNames = {
    blob: 'privatelink.blob.${environment().suffixes.storage}'
    sites: 'privatelink.azurewebsites.net'
    vault: 'privatelink.vaultcore.azure.net'
    monitor: 'privatelink.monitor.azure.com'
    oms: 'privatelink.oms.opinsights.azure.com'
    ods: 'privatelink.ods.opinsights.azure.com'
    agentsvc: 'privatelink.agentsvc.azure-automation.net'
  }
  // Named as regionStamp.bicep names them in the East US stamp.
  var names = {
    appServicePlan: 'asp-defenstack-${environmentName}-${regionCode}'
    bastion: 'bas-defenstack-${environmentName}-${regionCode}'
    bastionPublicIp: 'pip-bas-defenstack-${environmentName}-${regionCode}'
    hubVnet: 'vnet-defenstack-${environmentName}-${regionCode}-hub'
    spokeVnet: 'vnet-defenstack-${environmentName}-${regionCode}-spoke'
    amplsAsg: 'vnet-defenstack-${environmentName}-${regionCode}-spoke-ampls-asg'
    monitorPrivateEndpoint: 'ampls-defenstack-${environmentName}-${regionCode}-pe'
    // Distinct from the account's own <account>-pe (West US 3) and the standby's <account>-secondary-pe (blob_secondary).
    storagePrivateEndpoint: '${last(split(primaryStorageAccountId, '/'))}-${regionCode}-pe'
  }

  // Existing resources from main.bicep: the East US hub and spoke, and the shared layer in the global resource group.
  resource hubVnet 'Microsoft.Network/virtualNetworks@2025-09-01' existing = {
    name: names.hubVnet
    scope: resourceGroup(resourceGroupName)

    resource bastionSubnet 'subnets' existing = {
      name: 'AzureBastionSubnet'
    }
  }

  resource spokeVnet 'Microsoft.Network/virtualNetworks@2025-09-01' existing = {
    name: names.spokeVnet
    scope: resourceGroup(resourceGroupName)

    resource privateEndpointSubnet 'subnets' existing = {
      name: 'private-endpoints'
    }
  }

  resource amplsAsg 'Microsoft.Network/applicationSecurityGroups@2024-07-01' existing = {
    name: names.amplsAsg
    scope: resourceGroup(resourceGroupName)
  }

  resource workspace 'Microsoft.OperationalInsights/workspaces@2026-03-01' existing = {
    name: 'log-defenstack-${environmentName}'
    scope: resourceGroup(globalResourceGroupName)
  }

  resource privateLinkScope 'Microsoft.Insights/privateLinkScopes@2021-09-01' existing = {
    name: 'ampls-defenstack-${environmentName}'
    scope: resourceGroup(globalResourceGroupName)
  }

  // a. The warm-standby plan runs at primary capacity. Same name, SKU and tier; only the instance count changes.
  module appServicePlanScale 'modules/failover/appServicePlanScale.bicep' = if (failoverActive) {
    name: 'failover-app-service-plan-${regionCode}'
    scope: resourceGroup(resourceGroupName)
    params: {
      location: location
      appServicePlanName: names.appServicePlan
      capacity: appServicePlanCapacity
    }
  }

  // b. Admin access in East US for the restored jump host (runbook 08). The hub's AzureBastionSubnet exists from Phase 1,
  // and governance.bicep already allows the pip-bas-…-eus public IP. The VPN gateway is not deployed: it takes too long
  // for the one-hour RTO.
  module bastion 'modules/bastion.bicep' = if (failoverActive) {
    name: 'failover-bastion-${regionCode}'
    scope: resourceGroup(resourceGroupName)
    params: {
      location: location
      bastionName: names.bastion
      publicIpName: names.bastionPublicIp
      subnetId: hubVnet::bastionSubnet.id
      availabilityZones: [
        '1'
        '2'
        '3'
      ]
      logAnalyticsWorkspaceId: workspace.id
    }
  }

  // c. A second Azure Monitor endpoint in the East US spoke. Its zone group overwrites the shared Azure Monitor records,
  // so every VNet resolves the scope to East US. Failback deletes it and redeploys the primary endpoint (runbook 08).
  module monitorPrivateEndpoint 'modules/monitorPrivateEndpoint.bicep' = if (failoverActive) {
    name: 'failover-monitor-private-endpoint-${regionCode}'
    scope: resourceGroup(resourceGroupName)
    params: {
      location: location
      privateLinkScopeId: privateLinkScope.id
      privateLinkScopeName: privateLinkScope.name
      privateEndpointName: names.monitorPrivateEndpoint
      privateEndpointSubnetId: spokeVnet::privateEndpointSubnet.id
      privateDnsZoneIds: toObject(
        items(privateDnsZoneNames),
        zone => zone.key,
        zone => resourceId(subscription().subscriptionId, globalResourceGroupName, 'Microsoft.Network/privateDnsZones', zone.value)
      )
      applicationSecurityGroupId: amplsAsg.id
    }
  }

  // d. Blob writes from East US. The account's own endpoint sits in the unreachable West US 3 spoke, so this endpoint in
  // the East US spoke reaches the account once it has failed over. Its zone group overwrites the account's record in the
  // shared blob zone; failback deletes it and the next pipeline deploy restores the West US 3 record (runbook 08). The
  // write role stays a manual, PIM-gated runbook step, so the standby is read-only outside DR (ADR-020, ADR-032).
  module storagePrivateEndpoint 'modules/failover/storagePrivateEndpoint.bicep' = if (failoverActive) {
    name: 'failover-storage-private-endpoint-${regionCode}'
    scope: resourceGroup(resourceGroupName)
    params: {
      location: location
      storageAccountId: primaryStorageAccountId
      privateEndpointName: names.storagePrivateEndpoint
      privateEndpointSubnetId: spokeVnet::privateEndpointSubnet.id
      blobPrivateDnsZoneId: resourceId(
        subscription().subscriptionId,
        globalResourceGroupName,
        'Microsoft.Network/privateDnsZones',
        privateDnsZoneNames.blob
      )
    }
  }

  output resourceGroupName string = resourceGroupName
  output appServicePlanName string = failoverActive ? names.appServicePlan : ''
  output bastionName string = failoverActive ? names.bastion : ''
  output monitorPrivateEndpointId string = failoverActive ? monitorPrivateEndpoint!.outputs.id : ''
  output storagePrivateEndpointId string = failoverActive ? storagePrivateEndpoint!.outputs.id : ''
  ```

  Create `params/failover.bicepparam` (a parameter file cannot call `subscription()`, so `primaryStorageAccountId` stays with the template default; do not set a literal ID):

  ```bicep
  using '../failover.bicep'

  // Regional failover (Phase 8): prod only, deployed by an operator during a declared West US 3 outage (runbook 08),
  // never by the pipeline. Run a what-if first; failback undoes these changes by hand and through the pipeline.
  param environmentName = 'prod'
  param location = 'eastus'
  // The prod primary plan runs 3 instances.
  param appServicePlanCapacity = 3
  param failoverActive = true
  // primaryStorageAccountId is not set here: a parameter file cannot call subscription(), so the template default names
  // the prod primary account as main.bicep does (rg-defenstack-prod-wus3, stwus3<uniqueString(subscription id, 'prod',
  // 'westus3')>). Override it with --parameters primaryStorageAccountId=<id> only if that account was renamed; the
  // East US blob endpoint is named <account>-eus-pe.
  ```

- [ ] **Step 6: Keep failover out of the deploy**

  Replace `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` (two negated path filters, each after the pattern it narrows; `failover.bicep` matches no include pattern):

  ````yaml
  name: deploy

  on:
    push:
      branches: [main]
      paths:
        - 'main.bicep'
        - 'modules/**'
        # Governance is deployed by an operator (runbook 07); a workload redeploy would only reset the JIT NSG rules.
        - '!modules/governance/**'
        # Failover is deployed by an operator during a declared outage (runbook 08); its root template matches no pattern here.
        - '!modules/failover/**'
        - 'params/**'
        - '!params/governance.bicepparam'
        - '!params/failover.bicepparam'
    workflow_dispatch:
      inputs:
        environment:
          description: Target environment
          type: choice
          options: [dev, prod]
          default: dev

  permissions:
    contents: read
    id-token: write

  concurrency:
    group: deploy-${{ inputs.environment || 'dev' }}
    cancel-in-progress: false

  env:
    BICEP_VERSION: v0.47.16
    DEPLOYMENT_LOCATION: westus3
    TARGET_ENV: ${{ inputs.environment || 'dev' }}

  jobs:
    # Prod: runs in the reviewer-gated prod environment (approve plan, read the what-if, then approve apply) so every prod token needs approval.
    # Dev: runs in dev-plan (no reviewers).
    plan:
      if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      environment: ${{ inputs.environment == 'prod' && 'prod' || 'dev-plan' }}
      steps:
        # Same check as apply: a prod plan without ALERT_EMAIL would show a what-if that apply then refuses (runbook 06).
        - name: Require ALERT_EMAIL for prod
          if: env.TARGET_ENV == 'prod'
          env:
            ALERT_EMAIL: ${{ vars.ALERT_EMAIL }}
          run: |
            if [ -z "$ALERT_EMAIL" ]; then
              echo "::error::Set the ALERT_EMAIL variable on the prod GitHub environment before deploying prod (runbook 06)."
              exit 1
            fi

        - uses: actions/checkout@v4

        - uses: azure/login@v2
          with:
            client-id: ${{ vars.AZURE_CLIENT_ID }}
            tenant-id: ${{ vars.AZURE_TENANT_ID }}
            subscription-id: ${{ vars.AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID }}

        - name: Install Bicep CLI
          run: |
            az config set bicep.use_binary_from_path=false
            az bicep install --version "$BICEP_VERSION"

        - name: Validate
          env:
            ALERT_EMAIL: ${{ vars.ALERT_EMAIL }}
          run: |
            az deployment sub validate \
              --location "$DEPLOYMENT_LOCATION" \
              --name "gh-${{ github.run_id }}-${{ github.run_attempt }}" \
              --parameters "params/${TARGET_ENV}.bicepparam" \
              --parameters alertEmail="$ALERT_EMAIL" \
              --output none

        - name: What-if
          env:
            ALERT_EMAIL: ${{ vars.ALERT_EMAIL }}
          run: |
            set -o pipefail
            az deployment sub what-if \
              --location "$DEPLOYMENT_LOCATION" \
              --name "gh-${{ github.run_id }}-${{ github.run_attempt }}" \
              --parameters "params/${TARGET_ENV}.bicepparam" \
              --parameters alertEmail="$ALERT_EMAIL" \
              --exclude-change-types Ignore NoChange 2>&1 \
              | sed -r 's/\x1B\[[0-9;]*[mK]//g' > whatif.txt
            {
              echo "### What-if: ${TARGET_ENV} (subscription scope, rg-defenstack-${TARGET_ENV}-*)"
              echo '```'
              head -c 60000 whatif.txt
              echo '```'
            } >> "$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY"

        - name: Keep the what-if for reviewers
          uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
          with:
            name: whatif-${{ inputs.environment || 'dev' }}
            path: whatif.txt
            retention-days: 14
            if-no-files-found: error
            overwrite: true

    # Gated by the target environment's required reviewers (prod); reviewers read the plan job summary first.
    apply:
      needs: plan
      if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
      runs-on: ubuntu-latest
      environment: ${{ inputs.environment || 'dev' }}
      steps:
        # Prod alerts must reach someone: stop before any Azure call when the prod environment has no ALERT_EMAIL (runbook 06).
        - name: Require ALERT_EMAIL for prod
          if: env.TARGET_ENV == 'prod'
          env:
            ALERT_EMAIL: ${{ vars.ALERT_EMAIL }}
          run: |
            if [ -z "$ALERT_EMAIL" ]; then
              echo "::error::Set the ALERT_EMAIL variable on the prod GitHub environment before deploying prod (runbook 06)."
              exit 1
            fi

        - uses: actions/checkout@v4

        - uses: azure/login@v2
          with:
            client-id: ${{ vars.AZURE_CLIENT_ID }}
            tenant-id: ${{ vars.AZURE_TENANT_ID }}
            subscription-id: ${{ vars.AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID }}

        - name: Install Bicep CLI
          run: |
            az config set bicep.use_binary_from_path=false
            az bicep install --version "$BICEP_VERSION"

        # Application secrets come from the environment secret KEYVAULT_SECRETS_JSON and are written to every regional vault (ADR-019).
        - name: Prepare Key Vault secrets
          shell: pwsh
          env:
            KEYVAULT_SECRETS_JSON: ${{ secrets.KEYVAULT_SECRETS_JSON }}
          run: ./scripts/ConvertTo-KeyVaultSecretsParameter.ps1 -Json $env:KEYVAULT_SECRETS_JSON -OutFile "$env:RUNNER_TEMP/keyvault-secrets.json"

        - name: Deploy
          env:
            ALERT_EMAIL: ${{ vars.ALERT_EMAIL }}
          run: |
            az deployment sub create \
              --location "$DEPLOYMENT_LOCATION" \
              --name "gh-${{ github.run_id }}-${{ github.run_attempt }}" \
              --parameters "params/${TARGET_ENV}.bicepparam" \
              --parameters keyVaultSecrets=@"$RUNNER_TEMP/keyvault-secrets.json" \
              --parameters alertEmail="$ALERT_EMAIL" \
              --output table

        - name: Remove the secrets file
          if: always()
          run: rm -f "$RUNNER_TEMP/keyvault-secrets.json"

        # The approval wait can outlast the first login's token (up to 15 minutes per app), so refresh it immediately
        # before the step that needs it.
        - name: Refresh Azure login before approval
          uses: azure/login@v2
          with:
            client-id: ${{ vars.AZURE_CLIENT_ID }}
            tenant-id: ${{ vars.AZURE_TENANT_ID }}
            subscription-id: ${{ vars.AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID }}

        # Front Door reaches each App Service over Private Link; its connections stay Pending until approved (runbook 04).
        - name: Approve Front Door private endpoint connections
          shell: pwsh
          run: |
            $outputs = az deployment sub show --name "gh-${{ github.run_id }}-${{ github.run_attempt }}" --query properties.outputs --output json | ConvertFrom-Json
            $ids = $outputs.appServiceIds.value
            $profileName = $outputs.frontDoorProfileName.value
            $resourceGroupName = $outputs.globalResourceGroupName.value
            if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0 -or -not $ids) { throw 'Could not read appServiceIds from the deployment outputs.' }
            if (-not $profileName) { throw 'Could not read frontDoorProfileName from the deployment outputs.' }
            if (-not $resourceGroupName) { throw 'Could not read globalResourceGroupName from the deployment outputs.' }
            ./scripts/Approve-FrontDoorPrivateEndpoints.ps1 -AppServiceId $ids -FrontDoorProfileName $profileName -FrontDoorResourceGroupName $resourceGroupName
  ````

- [ ] **Step 7: Build, lint, test, YAML, PSRule**

  ```bash
  bicep build main.bicep
  bicep build governance.bicep
  git checkout -- governance.json
  bicep build failover.bicep
  for f in main.bicep governance.bicep failover.bicep modules/*.bicep modules/governance/*.bicep modules/failover/*.bicep; do bicep lint "$f" || echo "LINT FAIL $f"; done
  git status --short main.json governance.json failover.json
  ```

  Expected: no `LINT FAIL`; a new `failover.json`; `governance.json` unchanged. `main.json` changes in five places only: the two template hashes, the new `privateEndpointName` parameter, and three name expressions that now read `parameters('privateEndpointName')`, which still evaluates to `ampls-defenstack-<env>-pe`. `failover.json` is not languageVersion 2.0, so the tests find its modules by deployment name. Then run the full suite: `Tests Passed: 783, Failed: 0`. The YAML check prints `YAML-OK`. PSRule: 0 failures, 1448 rules processed. `params/failover.bicepparam` alone accounts for 54: the plan, Bastion and its public IP, and both endpoints. The plan's `Azure.AppService.AvailabilityZone` is covered by the existing ADR-008 suppression.
