  Create `docs/decisions/ADR-030-backup-vaults-immutability-and-lock-later.md`:

  ```markdown
  # ADR-030: Backup vaults start reversible: immutability unlocked, soft delete not always-on, locked after the first drill

  ## Context
  Phase 8 adds a Recovery Services vault to every region stamp (`rsv-defenstack-<env>-<region>`) and, in prod West US 3, a Backup vault for blob vaulted backup (`bv-defenstack-prod-wus3`, `ADR-031`). The spec (§3 "Data and application resilience") asks for a Recovery Services vault per region. Azure Backup's strongest posture makes a vault's immutability **Locked** and its soft delete **always-on**. Both changes are irreversible: "Once you enable the always-on state for soft delete, it can't be disabled for that vault" ([enhanced soft delete](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/backup-azure-enhanced-soft-delete-about)), and "Immutability locking is irreversible" ([immutable vault](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/backup-azure-immutable-vault-concept#making-immutability-irreversible)). Locking a vault before the backup design is proven would freeze any mistake in a policy's retention or a protected item, because a locked vault refuses every operation that could shorten retention or delete recovery points.

  PSRule for Azure's `Azure.RSV.ReplicationAlert` expects each Recovery Services vault to have a `vaults/replicationAlertSettings` child, which configures the classic Site Recovery email to the subscription owners.

  ## Decision
  - **Every vault starts reversible.**
    - Recovery Services vaults: `softDeleteSettings.softDeleteState: 'Enabled'`, 14 days, `enhancedSecurityState: 'Enabled'`, and `immutabilitySettings.state: 'Unlocked'` with no `configuration` (immutability follows each policy's retention).
    - The Backup vault: `softDeleteSettings.state: 'On'`, 14 days, and `immutabilitySettings.state: 'Unlocked'`.
    - The two resource types spell these differently (`softDeleteState`/`AlwaysON` against `state`/`AlwaysOn`), so the modules keep separate blocks.
  - **Locking happens after the first successful drill** (runbook 08 §5.3), and only with sign-off recorded in this ADR. Immutability goes to `Locked` and soft delete to `AlwaysON` (Recovery Services vault) or `AlwaysOn` (Backup vault) on every vault. The change is made **in Bicep** (`modules/recoveryServicesVault.bicep`, `modules/blobBackupVault.bicep`, and their tests) and deployed through the pipeline. Setting it only in the portal or with the CLI would leave the templates saying `Unlocked`, and the next deploy would try to unlock the vault and fail.
  - **Storage is fixed before anything is protected.**
    - Each Recovery Services vault's `backupstorageconfig/vaultstorageconfig` sets `GeoRedundant` with `crossRegionRestoreFlag: true`, and the protected item depends on it. The storage type locks once an item is registered.
    - The Backup vault's `storageSettings` is `VaultStore`/`GeoRedundant`.
  - **Alerts use Azure Monitor, not the classic channel.**
    - Every vault sets `monitoringSettings.azureMonitorAlertSettings.alertsForAllJobFailures: 'Enabled'`.
    - Recovery Services vaults turn the classic `alertsForCriticalOperations` and `emailNotificationsForSiteRecovery` off.
    - One alert processing rule per environment, `apr-defenstack-<env>-backup` (`MonitorService` = `Azure Backup`), adds the platform action group, so backup alerts reach `ALERT_EMAIL` with every other alert (runbook 06, runbook 08 §5.5).
  - **`Azure.RSV.ReplicationAlert` is excluded** in `ps-rule.yaml`, with the comment `# ADR-030: no Site Recovery replication exists; backup alerts are Azure Monitor alerts routed to the action group.` The rule protects Site Recovery replication, and this platform replicates nothing with Site Recovery (the warm standby is a deployed stamp, `ADR-008`). Adding `replicationAlertSettings` would add back a classic email channel that this ADR turns off. Backup job failures are covered by the Azure Monitor alerts above.
  - **Prod vaults carry a `CanNotDelete` lock** (`<vault>-lck`, `enableDeleteLock`), like every other prod resource. No lock is ever placed on `AzureBackupRG_<region>_1`, where Azure Backup keeps instant-restore snapshots and must be able to delete them.

  ## Consequences
  - Until the lock, a principal with Backup Contributor on a vault can disable immutability or soft delete, and then delete recovery points. Soft delete still keeps deleted backup data for 14 days, and the vault lock stops the vault itself from being deleted. Accepted for the weeks before the first drill.
  - After the lock, retention can only grow. A shorter policy needs a new policy name and a new vault, and the old recovery points live until they expire. Locked immutability also blocks stopping protection with delete data.
  - Immutability does not cover operational (local) blob backups; the platform uses vaulted backup only.
  - The PSRule baseline stays at zero failures with one more exclusion, justified here; a test pins the exclusion and its comment.

  ## Revisit when
  The first quarterly drill succeeds (runbook 08 §5.3): record the date, the sign-off and the commit that locks the vaults here. Revisit the exclusion if Site Recovery is ever adopted.
  ```

  Create `docs/decisions/ADR-031-blob-vaulted-backup.md`:

  ```markdown
  # ADR-031: Blob vaulted backup of the prod application container

  ## Context
  The prod application account (`st<wus3><suffix>`, `Standard_RAGZRS`, `ADR-020`) already has soft delete, versioning, change feed and point-in-time restore. All of these live **in the account**. They recover from mistakes, but not from a compromise that deletes the account or turns its protection off. The user asked for an offsite copy in addition to the spec's Recovery Services vaults. Azure Backup's **vaulted** backup for blobs copies the chosen containers into a Backup vault on a schedule and keeps them under the vault's own soft delete, immutability and lock (`ADR-030`).

  ## Decision
  - **Where:** prod West US 3 only. `regionStamp.bicep` gates it with `enableBlobVaultedBackup`, default `environmentName == 'prod' && regionRole == 'primary'`. Dev and the East US standby have no Backup vault.
  - **Vault:** `Microsoft.DataProtection/backupVaults@2026-06-01`, named `bv-defenstack-prod-wus3`.
    - Identity: system-assigned only. "Backup vaults with User-Assigned Managed Identity (UAMI) aren't compatible with Azure Blob Vaulted backups" ([support matrix](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/blob-backup-support-matrix)).
    - Storage: `VaultStore`/`GeoRedundant`.
    - Security settings: soft delete `On`, 14 days, and immutability `Unlocked`, until the first drill (`ADR-030`).
    - Cross-region restore: `Disabled`, because blobs are not in the cross-region restore support table ([support matrix](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/backup-support-matrix#cross-region-restore)).
    - Alerts, diagnostics and lock: Azure Monitor job-failure alerts, the four Backup vault diagnostic tables (resource-specific), and a `CanNotDelete` lock.
  - **Policy `blob-daily-vaulted`:** one `AzureBackupRule` at 02:00 UTC daily into `VaultStore`, and one `AzureRetentionRule` keeping 30 days (`P30D`). Azure Backup does not support modifying an existing policy, so a different schedule or retention needs a **new policy name** and an update of the backup instance to point at it.
  - **Access:** the vault's identity gets **Storage Account Backup Contributor** (`e5e2a7ff-d759-4cd2-bb51-3152d37e2eb1`) on the account only, under a deterministic `guid(account, vault, role)` name. The pipeline may assign it because the role is in `New-GitHubDeploymentIdentity.ps1`'s delegatable list and ABAC condition (runbook 00b).
  - **Backup instance:** named after the account. It protects the containers listed by `regionStamp.bicep`, which is `def-blob` (`storage.outputs.blobContainerName`), and depends on the role assignment.
  - **Datasource parameters: `BlobBackupDatasourceParameters`, not `AdlsBlobBackupDatasourceParameters`.**
    - Our Phase 8 research proposed the Data Lake name as a "correction". That correction was wrong. The REST guide's request for vaulted backup with an explicit container list uses `"objectType": "BlobBackupDatasourceParameters"` with `containersList` ([REST: back up blobs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/backup-azure-dataprotection-use-rest-api-backup-blobs)). `AdlsBlobBackupDatasourceParameters` is its subtype for Data Lake (hierarchical namespace) accounts, and this account has no hierarchical namespace.
    - Bicep 0.47.16's types list only the subtypes, so that one line carries `#disable-next-line BCP036`, with a comment.
    - **Fallback**, only if ARM rejects the base type live: switch to `AdlsBlobBackupDatasourceParameters` and record it here (runbook 08 §9).
  - **Effects on the protected account, checked against this repository.** The REST guide warns that "Once a storage account is configured for blobs backup, a few capabilities are affected, such as change feed and delete lock" ([REST: back up blobs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/backup-azure-dataprotection-use-rest-api-backup-blobs)). Per "Effects on backed-up storage accounts" ([configure and manage](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/blob-backup-configure-manage#effects-on-backed-up-storage-accounts)):
    - **Vaulted backup** creates **object replication** rules on the account. It also turns on **versioning and change feed**, because object replication needs them.
    - **Operational backup** (not used here) would instead turn on point-in-time restore and blob soft delete with policy-driven retention, and apply a **Backup-owned delete lock** to the account.
    - Stopping protection removes the object replication policy automatically, unless custom locks exist, in which case it must be removed by hand. It never turns off point-in-time restore, versioning or change feed.

    **No conflict in this repository:**
    - `storage.bicep` already enables versioning and change feed (14-day retention), so Azure Backup finds them on.
    - `storage.bicep` does not manage object replication policies, so a redeploy leaves Backup's policy alone.
    - No template puts a lock on the storage account or its resource group (the prod locks are on the VNets, Key Vault, firewall, zones, workspace, Front Door and the vaults).
    - Operational backup is not configured, so no Backup-owned delete lock appears.

    The compliant option is therefore the current one: no change to `storage.bicep`.
  - **The one real interaction is failover.** A **planned** storage failover is refused while change feed, object replication or point-in-time restore is on ([failover FAQ](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/storage/common/storage-failover-faq#what-are-the-conflicting-features-or-scenarios-for-failovers)). So failback pauses blob backup, deletes the object replication policy, and turns off point-in-time restore and change feed before the planned failover. The pipeline then restores them, and protection resumes (runbook 08 §5.2).

  ## Consequences
  - **Limits:**
    - Restores go to a **different** storage account only, which must not already hold a container with the same name.
    - There is no cross-region restore: during a West US 3 outage the vaulted copies are not restorable until the region returns. RA-GZRS read access and the storage failover (runbook 08) cover that case instead.
    - The recovery point is at most one day old (one backup a day).
    - At most 1000 containers can be protected.
  - **Costs:** protected-instance and vault-storage charges, plus object replication traffic on the source (`docs/cost.md` "Phase 8 delta").
  - **Change feed and object replication add append blobs** to the account. That makes it ineligible for the Geo priority replication SLA ([Geo priority replication](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/storage/common/storage-redundancy-priority-replication#sla-eligibility-and-exclusions)), so the 15-minute storage RPO stays an expectation, not an SLA (runbook 08 §1).
  - **Live-only risks**, with fallbacks in runbook 08 §9:
    - backup with `publicNetworkAccess: 'Disabled'`;
    - a role-propagation race on the first deploy;
    - idempotent re-PUTs of the policy;
    - the change-feed retention that `storage.bicep` re-asserts.

  ## Revisit when
  Azure adds cross-region restore for blobs, when the containers or the retention change (new policy name), or if ARM rejects the base datasource type.
  ```

  Create `docs/decisions/ADR-032-failover-entry-point-and-pipeline-freeze.md`:

  ```markdown
  # ADR-032: Regional failover is a separate, operator-run entry point, and the pipeline freezes during DR

  ## Context
  Prod runs a warm standby in East US (`ADR-008`). In steady state the standby is cut down: one App Service instance, no Bastion and no VPN gateway, and Azure Monitor traffic crosses the hub peering to the single AMPLS endpoint in West US 3 (`ADR-022`). The target for a declared West US 3 outage is **RTO 1 hour, RPO 15 minutes**, and an operator drives the failover from runbook 08.

  The pipeline cannot do this:
  - `main.bicep` deploys every stamp, starting with the West US 3 one, so a run during the outage fails in the unreachable region.
  - Every successful run puts the standby back to its steady state: the East US plan to 1 instance, the West US 3 Front Door origin back to `Enabled`, and the primary storage account back in `westus3`.

  ## Decision
  - **`failover.bicep` is a third root entry point** (`targetScope = 'subscription'`, with `params/failover.bicepparam`, prod only). An operator deploys it with PIM-activated rights (runbook 08 §4 step 5). `main.bicep` never references it, `deploy.yml` never deploys it, and its path filter ignores `modules/failover/**` and `params/failover.bicepparam`. CI lints, builds, tests and drift-checks it (`failover.json` is committed) and runs PSRule on its parameter file, as it does for governance (`ADR-026`).
  - **It deploys into `rg-defenstack-prod-eus` only:**
    - The East US App Service plan, at `appServicePlanCapacity` instances (default 3, the primary's count). It uses the prod SKU `P2V3`/`PremiumV3` and the same name, so only the instance count changes, and the app is not redeployed. Zone redundancy stays off, because a plan's zone redundancy is fixed at creation. The existing ADR-008 PSRule suppression for `asp-defenstack-prod-eus` covers this.
    - Bastion `bas-defenstack-prod-eus` from `modules/bastion.bicep`, in the hub's existing `AzureBastionSubnet`. Its public IP `pip-bas-defenstack-prod-eus` is already in the governance allowlist. The VPN gateway is **not** deployed: it takes too long for the RTO, and Bastion is enough to reach a jump host restored into East US.
    - A second Azure Monitor endpoint, `ampls-defenstack-prod-eus-pe`, from `modules/monitorPrivateEndpoint.bicep` (its new `privateEndpointName` parameter defaults to the Phase 6 name, so `main.bicep`'s endpoint is unchanged). Its zone group rewrites the shared Azure Monitor records to East US IPs. Microsoft documents that with several endpoints in one DNS, "only the last one set in the DNS applies" ([AMPLS design](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-monitor/fundamentals/private-link-design#plan-by-network-topology)).
    - An East US blob endpoint, `<account>-eus-pe`, from `modules/failover/storagePrivateEndpoint.bicep`. It has the same shape as the stamp's storage endpoint in `privateConnectivity.bicep`: group `blob`, in the East US spoke's `private-endpoints` subnet, connected to the prod primary app account. The account's own endpoint `<account>-pe` sits in the West US 3 spoke, which is unreachable during the outage, so without this endpoint the East US app could not write to the account after it fails over. Its zone group overwrites the account's A record in the shared `privatelink.blob.core.windows.net` zone, as the Azure Monitor endpoint does for its records. The name differs from `<account>-pe` and from the standby's `<account>-secondary-pe` (`blob_secondary`).
    - The account ID is the `primaryStorageAccountId` parameter. A parameter file cannot call `subscription()`, so its default computes the ID as `regionStamp.bicep` names the account: `stwus3` plus `uniqueString(subscription().id, 'prod', 'westus3')`, in `rg-defenstack-prod-wus3`. A test pins the parity.
  - **The write role stays a manual, PIM-gated runbook step, outside Bicep.** During DR, one command grants **Storage Blob Data Contributor** to the East US app's identity on `def-blob`, and failback removes it (runbook 08 §4 step 6, §5.2 step 3). The warm standby holds only Storage Blob Data Reader in normal operation (`ADR-020`), and a stray `failover.bicep` run cannot widen that.
  - **It only reads from the global resource group:** the workspace, the AMPLS and the shared zones are `existing` references, and the endpoints' zone groups write records into the zones.
  - **Nothing targets West US 3.** Every module deploys into `rg-defenstack-prod-eus`. West US 3 appears only as a referenced resource ID: the storage account in the blob endpoint's `privateLinkServiceId` (the `primaryStorageAccountId` default). Tests pin both: each deployment's scope, and that `wus3` and `westus3` appear nowhere in `failover.json` outside that default.
  - **`failoverActive` (default `true`) gates every module.** `false` deploys nothing, so an accidental run outside DR is harmless.
  - **Pipeline freeze.** From the outage declaration until failback step 9, the `deploy` workflow is disabled (`gh workflow disable deploy`), no pull request merges to `main`, and nobody dispatches a prod deploy. The failback re-enables the workflow only for operator-dispatched prod runs, then lifts the freeze (runbook 08 §5.2). A pipeline run during DR would:
    - fail in West US 3;
    - scale East US back to 1 instance;
    - re-enable the dead origin;
    - try to move the failed-over storage account back to `westus3`.

  ## Consequences
  - Failover is not reviewed in a pull request at the time it runs. The template is, and the operator runs `what-if` first.
  - Nothing in `failover.bicep` is removed automatically. Failback removes the DR write grant, then deletes the East US blob endpoint, the East US Azure Monitor endpoint and Bastion by hand. The next prod pipeline run scales the plan back to 1, re-enables the West US 3 origin and re-PUTs the primary endpoints' zone groups, restoring their West US 3 records in the shared Azure Monitor and blob zones (runbook 08 §5.2). Whether that re-PUT rewrites a record another zone group overwrote is unverified, so runbook 08 §5.2 step 4 checks the blob record and §9 gives the fallback.
  - **The shared zones, the AMPLS, the workspace, Front Door and the action group live in `rg-defenstack-prod-global`, which runbook 01 creates in `westus3`.** A resource group's metadata is stored in its region. Microsoft states that ARM "automatically reroutes ARM control plane requests to a backup region" when that region is unavailable ([ARM overview](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-resource-manager/management/overview#which-location-should-i-use-for-my-resource-group)), but this platform has not exercised that, so it is **unverified**. If the East US endpoints' zone group writes fail during an outage, the fallback is either of these, until the global resource group is reachable (runbook 08 §9):
    - a temporary conditional-forwarder or hosts entry for the Azure Monitor FQDNs and the account's blob FQDN on the East US firewall DNS proxy, pointing at the East US endpoints' IPs;
    - creating the A records by hand once ARM accepts writes again.
  - The pipeline freeze also delays unrelated changes. Emergency fixes during DR are made by hand in East US and back-ported to Bicep after failback.

  ## Revisit when
  The platform moves the global resource group to a non-failing region or splits it per region, adopts an active-active design, or Microsoft documents automatic zone-group behaviour for this case.
  ```

  Create `docs/decisions/ADR-033-recovery-services-vault-public-access-denied-without-private-endpoint.md`:

  ```markdown
  # ADR-033: Recovery Services vaults deny public network access and have no private endpoint

  ## Context
  The platform keeps every PaaS service off public networks (spec §3, the Phase 7 deny policies). A Recovery Services vault exposes `publicNetworkAccess`. Private endpoints for Azure Backup exist, but each needs about 11 private IPs and three more DNS zones (`privatelink.<geo>.backup.windowsazure.com`, blob and queue). They can be created only on a vault that has never had an item protected or registered ([private endpoints](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/private-endpoints-overview#considerations-before-you-start)). The only workload in these vaults is the Azure VM backup of the jump host.

  Microsoft's guidance for that workload:
  - "A VM backup doesn't require you to allow access to any IPs or FQDNs. So, it doesn't require private endpoints for backup and restore of disks." ([scenarios](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/private-endpoints-overview#recommended-and-supported-scenarios))
  - "If public access is denied and a private endpoint isn't enabled, backups succeed, but restore operations fail for all workloads except Virtual Machines. However, Virtual Machine item-level recovery also fails." ([deny public network access](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/private-endpoints#deny-public-network-access-to-the-vault))

  ## Decision
  - Every Recovery Services vault sets `publicNetworkAccess: 'Disabled'` and has **no private endpoint**.
  - Recovery of the jump host uses full VM or disk restore only (runbook 08 §5.4), never item-level (file) recovery.
  - No Backup DNS zones are added to the shared zone set.

  ## Consequences
  - **Supported:** VM backup, full VM restore and disk restore, including cross-region restore to East US.
  - **Not available:** file-level recovery from a VM recovery point. Fallback: restore the disks and attach them to a VM to copy files.
  - **Adding a private endpoint later needs a new vault.** A vault that has protected an item cannot get one. The steps would be: create the vault, stop protection on the old one (retain data), protect in the new one, and let the old recovery points expire.
  - **Not proved by a live run.** Each live risk below has its fallback in runbook 08 §9:
    - The jump host is the first VM protected this way, so backup and restore are not yet proven.
    - Backup and restore work with a denied vault and no private endpoint (Microsoft's documented behaviour, but untested here).
    - Restore staging through the East US account, which has public access disabled.

  ## Revisit when
  A workload that needs private endpoints (SQL Server or SAP HANA in a VM, the MARS agent) is added, or item-level recovery becomes a requirement.
  ```

  **`README.md`, edit 3 of 3.** Find:

  ```markdown

  Resource API versions use the newest stable version that is both available from the target Azure provider and typed by the installed Bicep CLI. The Azure provider can expose newer versions before the local Bicep type catalog supports them; using those versions would remove compile-time property validation. Review provider metadata and the Bicep CLI catalog together before upgrading, then run lint, build, Azure deployment validation, and what-if. The only preview versions are for resource types or scopes with no GA version, each recorded with a fallback: the subscription Activity Log setting (`ADR-025`), and policy exemptions and the Defender for Cloud security contact (`ADR-029`).

  ```

  **`docs/architecture/overview.md`, edit 2 of 5.** Find:

  ```markdown

  What later phases add (not present after Phase 7):

  | Phase | Adds |
  |---|---|
  | 8 | Recovery Services vaults and the backup job alert, DR traffic over the hub-to-hub peering (Phase 6 carries only monitoring), the regional-failover runbook |
  ```

  Replace with:

  ```markdown

  **Phase 8** added backup and disaster recovery. Prod's target for a West US 3 regional outage is RTO 1 hour and RPO 15 minutes.
  - **Recovery Services vaults.** Every stamp has a vault `rsv-defenstack-<env>-<region>`: geo-redundant with cross-region restore, public access denied with no private endpoint (`ADR-033`), and soft delete and immutability left reversible until the first drill (`ADR-030`). The jump host has an Enhanced daily VM backup.
  - **Blob vaulted backup.** Prod West US 3 copies `def-blob` daily into the Backup vault `bv-defenstack-prod-wus3` (`ADR-031`).
  - **Backup alerts.** An alert processing rule, `apr-defenstack-<env>-backup`, sends Azure Backup alerts to the platform action group.
  - **Failover entry point.** A third entry point, `failover.bicep`, is run by an operator during a declared outage, never by the pipeline (`ADR-032`). It scales the East US plan to 3 instances, adds an East US Bastion, adds an East US Azure Monitor endpoint that re-points the shared Azure Monitor records, and adds an East US blob endpoint to the primary app account so the standby can write to it once the account fails over. The write role itself stays a manual, PIM-gated runbook step (`ADR-020`).
  - **Operator runbook.** The operator also disables the West US 3 Front Door origin, fails the storage account over, switches the workspace over and freezes the pipeline. Failback is planned.
  - **The hub peering** still carries only Azure Monitor traffic.

  See [runbook 08](../runbooks/08-dr-failover.md).

  ```

  **`docs/cost.md`, edit 1 of 1.** Find:

  ```markdown

  ## Dominant future cost drivers
  ```

  Replace with:

  ```markdown

  ## Phase 8 delta
  Phase 8 adds backup in every stamp and the operator-run regional failover
  (runbook 08). As with every phase, **fill in the actual dollar estimate from
  the Pricing Calculator; do not invent prices.** Each row names the billing
  unit. Read the current rate on the Azure Backup pricing page
  (https://azure.microsoft.com/pricing/details/backup/) or the service's own
  page, marked **confirm on the pricing page** below.

  | Environment | Cost driver | What changed / why it costs more |
  |---|---|---|
  | Both | Recovery Services vault: Azure VM protected instance | Per protected VM per month, tiered by the VM's protected size: the jump host only, when `enableVirtualMachine = true` (**confirm on the pricing page**). A vault with nothing protected costs nothing |
  | Both | Recovery Services vault: backup storage | Per GB-month of vaulted recovery points at **GRS** rates (`GeoRedundant`, with cross-region restore): 30 daily and 12 weekly points of the jump host's disks (**confirm on the pricing page**). Cross-region restore itself is not billed separately when it is not used; a restore adds data transfer |
  | Both | Instant-restore snapshots | Per GB-month of managed-disk snapshots kept 7 days in `AzureBackupRG_<region>_1` (Enhanced policy), billed as snapshot storage (**confirm on the pricing page**) |
  | Prod | Backup vault: blob protected instance | Per protected storage account per month, sized by the protected data (`def-blob`) (**confirm on the pricing page**) |
  | Prod | Backup vault: vault storage | Per GB-month of vaulted blob recovery points (`VaultStore`, **GRS**), 30 days of daily points (**confirm on the pricing page**) |
  | Prod | Object replication on the source account | Change feed and versioning were already on (Phase 0). The new cost is object replication: read transactions on the source and the replicated data. Versions retained for replication add stored capacity (**confirm on the pricing page**) |
  | Both | Backup diagnostics and alerts | Resource-specific backup tables ingested into the workspace, per GB (small; Sentinel rates, Phase 6). Built-in backup alerts and the alert processing rule have no charge (**confirm on the pricing page**) |
  | Prod, **DR only** | Azure Bastion Standard in East US | Per hour per Bastion host plus per extra scale unit (2 units), and outbound data per GB, from `failover.bicep` until failback deletes it (**confirm on the pricing page**). Plus one Standard public IP per hour |
  | Prod, **DR only** | App Service plan P2V3 in East US | Per instance-hour: 2 more instances than steady state (3 instead of 1) while failed over (**confirm on the pricing page**); also 2 more Defender for App Service instances (Phase 7) |
  | Prod, **DR only** | AMPLS private endpoint in East US | Per endpoint-hour plus inbound and outbound GB processed (**confirm on the pricing page**) |
  | Prod, **DR only** | Blob private endpoint `<primary account>-eus-pe` in East US | Per endpoint-hour plus inbound and outbound GB processed, from `failover.bicep` until failback deletes it (**confirm on the pricing page**) |
  | Prod, **DR only** | Storage failover and re-replication | An unplanned failover leaves the account LRS. Re-enabling RA-GRS, then the RA-GZRS conversion after failback, bills geo-replication data transfer per GB for the full re-copy (**confirm on the pricing page**) |
  | Prod, **DR only** | Workspace switchover | No charge for the switchover itself; workspace replication is already billed per replicated GB since Phase 6 (**confirm on the pricing page**) |

  **Estimate:** fill in from the Pricing Calculator: Azure Backup (Azure VM ×
  protected instances and GB, Azure Blob vaulted × protected GB), Storage
  (object replication transactions), and a separate DR scenario (Bastion
  Standard × hours, App Service P2V3 × 2 instances × hours, private endpoints ×2
  × hours) priced per day of outage. Measure after a month with Cost Analysis
  filtered to the service names "Backup" and "Storage" in
  `rg-defenstack-<env>-<region>`; DR-time costs appear in
  `rg-defenstack-prod-eus` only during a drill or a real failover.

  ## Dominant future cost drivers
  ```

  ```markdown
  ### 1a. Plan/apply split: why dev gets a `-plan` environment and prod does not
  ```

  Replace with:

  ```markdown

  **Phase 8 adds one delegatable role and changes the ABAC condition, so re-run the identity script before the first Phase 8 deploy.**
  - **The new role.** The pipeline now assigns **Storage Account Backup Contributor** (`e5e2a7ff-d759-4cd2-bb51-3152d37e2eb1`) to the prod Backup vault's identity on the application storage account (`ADR-031`). `scripts/New-GitHubDeploymentIdentity.ps1` includes it in its default `-DelegatableRoleDefinitionIds`, and the ABAC condition is built from that list.
  - **Re-applying the assignment.** An existing identity keeps the old condition, so re-apply it (§8, "Assigning an extra role from Bicep": delete the RBAC Administrator assignment on each resource group, then re-run the script). Otherwise the first Phase 8 prod deploy fails with `AuthorizationFailed` on that role assignment.
  - **No other new grants.** The Recovery Services and Backup vaults, their policies and protected items, the object replication policy that Azure Backup creates, and the alert processing rule all fall under the identity's resource-group `Contributor`.
  - **The pipeline does not run `failover.bicep`.** It is the operator's regional failover entry point (runbook 08, `ADR-032`). `deploy.yml`'s path filter also excludes `modules/failover/**` and `params/failover.bicepparam`. During a declared outage the pipeline is frozen (runbook 08 §5.1).
  ```

  **`docs/runbooks/01-deploy-stack.md`, edit 1 of 2.** Find:


  **`docs/runbooks/05-app-and-data.md`, edit 1 of 3.** Find:

  ```markdown

  **Warm-standby read path (prod only):**
  ```

  Replace with:

  ```markdown

  **Effects of blob vaulted backup on the prod primary account (Phase 8, `ADR-031`).** Microsoft warns that "once a storage account is configured for blobs backup, a few capabilities are affected, such as change feed and delete lock" ([REST: back up blobs](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/backup-azure-dataprotection-use-rest-api-backup-blobs)).
  - **What vaulted backup does to the account.** It creates an **object replication** policy, and it turns on **versioning and change feed** ([effects on backed-up storage accounts](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/backup/blob-backup-configure-manage#effects-on-backed-up-storage-accounts)). Both are already on here, and `storage.bicep` leaves the object replication policy alone.
  - **What it does not do.** The Backup-owned **delete lock** and the policy-driven point-in-time restore and soft delete retention come only with **operational** backup, which this platform does not use. No template locks the account.
  - **Stopping protection** removes the object replication policy, but leaves versioning, change feed and point-in-time restore on.
  - **The one interaction to plan for:** a **planned** account failover is refused while change feed, object replication or point-in-time restore is on. Runbook 08 §5.2 turns them off first, and the pipeline turns them back on.

  **Warm-standby read path (prod only):**
  ```

## After the last task (human, not the implementer)

A person follows runbook 00b, then runbook 08:

1. **Before the first Phase 8 deploy:** re-run `scripts/New-GitHubDeploymentIdentity.ps1` (runbook 00b), so the ABAC condition admits Storage Account Backup Contributor (Review Focus 2). Then deploy dev and prod through the pipeline. Check the §6 steady-state rows: three Recovery Services vaults, `bv-defenstack-prod-wus3` protecting `def-blob`, and `apr-defenstack-<env>-backup`. Watch the §9 live-only risks on the first run: the blob backup instance (Review Focus 1) and the protected item's container discovery. Paste the outputs into the PR.
2. **First quarterly drill** (§5.3): planned failovers only, then §4 steps 1, 2 and 4–8 with `failover.bicep`, failback with §5.2, the test alert through `apr-defenstack-prod-backup` (Review Focus 5), and one VM cross-region restore and one blob vaulted restore. Record the measured RTO and RPO in the drill log. The pipeline stays frozen from the declaration to failback step 9 (Review Focus 4).
3. **Only after that drill succeeds:** take the irreversible hardening (§5.3) as one reviewed Bicep change, with the ADR-030 sign-off: immutability `Locked` and always-on soft delete on all four vaults, deployed dev then prod through the pipeline (Review Focus 3). Record any fallback taken in the matching ADR.
